\|\mathrm{D}_{t_{j}}\bar{\Phi}_{t}^{-1}(x)\|\leq\bar{P}_{7}(M_{1,F},M_{2,F},1/m_{1,F})\exp[\bar{P}_{7}(M_{1,F},M_{2,F},1/m_{1,F})]\;.

(231)

Hence, there exists
P_{7}\in\mathtt{Poly}(3,\mathbb{R}_{+})
such that for any
x\in F^{-1}(0)
we have

\|\mathrm{D}_{t}\bar{\Phi}_{t}^{-1}(x)\|\leq P_{7}(M_{1,F},M_{2,F},1/m_{1,F})\exp[P_{7}(M_{1,F},M_{2,F},1/m_{1,F})]\;.

(232)

Next, note that for any
x\in F^{-1}(0)
,
t\in\operatorname{B}_{\infty}(0,\eta)
and
i\in\{1,\dots,p\}
we have

\mathrm{D}\bar{\Phi}_{t,i}^{-1}(x)=\mathrm{D}_{x}\Phi_{i}(-t_{i},\bar{\Phi}_{t,i+1}^{-1}(x))\mathrm{D}\bar{\Phi}_{t,i+1}^{-1}(x)\;,\qquad\mathrm{D}\bar{\Phi}_{t,p+1}^{-1}(x)=\operatorname{Id}\;.

(233)

Combining this result and (
229
), there exists

P_{8}\in\mathtt{Poly}(3,\mathbb{R}_{+})
such that for any
x\in F^{-1}(0)
and
i\in\{1,\dots,p\}

\|\mathrm{D}\bar{\Phi}_{t,i}^{-1}(x)\|\leq P_{8}(M_{1,F},M_{2,F},1/m_{1,F})\exp[P_{8}(M_{1,F},M_{2,F},1/m_{1,F})]\;.

(234)

In particular, since
\bar{\Phi}_{t}=\bar{\Phi}_{t,1}
we have for any

x\in F^{-1}(0)

\|\mathrm{D}\bar{\Phi}_{t}^{-1}(x)\|\leq P_{8}(M_{1,F},M_{2,F},1/m_{1,F})\exp[P_{8}(M_{1,F},M_{2,F},1/m_{1,F})]\;.

(235)

Finally, we give a uniform upper-bound on
\mathrm{D}_{t}\mathrm{D}\bar{\Phi}_{t}^{-1}
. Let
j\in\{1,\dots,p\}
. For any

i\in\{1,\dots,p\}
we distinguish three cases:

(i)

i>j
, then for any
x\in F^{-1}(0)
,
\mathrm{D}_{t_{j}}\mathrm{D}\bar{\Phi}_{t,i}^{-1}(x)=0
.

(ii)

i=j
, then for any
x\in F^{-1}(0)
,
\mathrm{D}_{t_{j}}\mathrm{D}\bar{\Phi}_{t,i}^{-1}(x)=-\mathrm{D}_{t,x}\Phi_{j}(-t_{j},\bar{\Phi}_{t,j+1}^{-1}(x))\mathrm{D}\bar{\Phi}_{t,j+1}^{-1}(x)
.

(iii)

i<j
, then for any
x\in F^{-1}(0)
, we have

\displaystyle\mathrm{D}_{t_{j}}\mathrm{D}\bar{\Phi}_{t,i}^{-1}(x)

\displaystyle=\mathrm{D}_{x}\Phi_{i}(-t_{i},\bar{\Phi}_{t,i+1}^{-1}(x))\mathrm{D}_{t_{j}}\mathrm{D}\bar{\Phi}_{t,i+1}^{-1}(x)

(236)

\displaystyle\qquad+\mathrm{D}_{x}^{2}\Phi_{i}(-t_{i},\bar{\Phi}_{t,i+1}^{-1}(x))(\mathrm{D}\bar{\Phi}_{t,i+1}^{-1}(x),\mathrm{D}_{t_{j}}\mathrm{D}\bar{\Phi}_{t,i+1}^{-1}(x))\;.

(237)

Using (
229
) and (
234
),
there exists
P_{9}\in\mathtt{Poly}(4,\mathbb{R}_{+})
such that for any

x\in F^{-1}(0)
we have

\|\mathrm{D}_{t}\mathrm{D}\bar{\Phi}_{t}^{-1}(x)\|\leq P_{9}(M_{1,F},M_{2,F},M_{3,F},1/m_{1,F})\exp[P_{9}(M_{1,F},M_{2,F},M_{3,F},1/m_{1,F})]\;.

(238)

Therefore, summarizing (
232
),
(
235
) and
(
238
), there exist

P_{7},P_{8}\in\mathtt{Poly}(\mathbb{R}_{+},3)
and
P_{9}\in\mathtt{Poly}(4,\mathbb{R}_{+})
such that
for any
x\in F^{-1}(0)
and
t\in\operatorname{B}_{\infty}(0,\eta)
we have

\displaystyle\|\mathrm{D}_{t}\bar{\Phi}_{t}^{-1}(x)\|\leq P_{7}(M_{1,F},M_{2,F},1/m_{1,F})\exp[P_{7}(M_{1,F},M_{2,F},1/m_{1,F})]\;,

(239)

\displaystyle\|\mathrm{D}\bar{\Phi}_{t}^{-1}(x)\|\leq P_{8}(M_{1,F},M_{2,F},1/m_{1,F})\exp[P_{8}(M_{1,F},M_{2,F},1/m_{1,F})]\;,

(240)

\displaystyle\|\mathrm{D}_{t}\mathrm{D}\bar{\Phi}_{t}^{-1}(x)\|\leq P_{9}(M_{1,F},M_{2,F},M_{3,F},1/m_{1,F})\exp[P_{9}(M_{1,F},M_{2,F},M_{3,F},1/m_{1,F})]\;.

(241)

(d)

Next, we use (
239
) to conclude the proof by providing
uniform upper-bounds on the differential
\chi
on
F^{-1}(0)
w.r.t.
t
,
where
\chi
given in (
194
). For any
x\in F^{-1}(0)
and

t\in\operatorname{B}_{\infty}(0,\eta)
we have

\displaystyle\mathrm{D}_{t}\chi(t,x)

\displaystyle=\mathrm{D}_{t}\varphi_{\Psi}(\bar{\Phi}_{t}^{-1}(x))\mathrm{J}F(\bar{\Phi}_{t}^{-1}(t))^{-1}\det(G(\bar{\Phi}_{t}^{-1}(x)))

(242)

\displaystyle\qquad+\varphi_{\Psi}(\bar{\Phi}_{t}^{-1}(x))\mathrm{D}_{t}\mathrm{J}F(\bar{\Phi}_{t}^{-1}(t))^{-1}\det(G(\bar{\Phi}_{t}^{-1}(x)))

(243)

\displaystyle\qquad+\varphi_{\Psi}(\bar{\Phi}_{t}^{-1}(x))\mathrm{J}F(\bar{\Phi}_{t}^{-1}(t))^{-1}\mathrm{D}_{t}\det(G(\bar{\Phi}_{t}^{-1}(x)))\;.

(244)

First, for any
x\in F^{-1}(0)
and

t\in\operatorname{B}_{\infty}(0,\eta)
we have

|\varphi_{\Psi}(\bar{\Phi}_{t}^{-1}(x))|\leq M_{0,\varphi_{\Psi}}\;,\qquad\mathrm{J}F(\bar{\Phi}_{t}^{-1}(x))^{-1}\leq 1/m_{1,F}\;.

(245)

In addition, using (
239
), there exists

P_{10}\in\mathtt{Poly}(3,\mathbb{R}_{+})
such that for any
x\in\mathsf{K}_{1}
and

t\in\operatorname{B}_{\infty}(0,\eta)

|\det(\mathrm{D}\bar{\Phi}_{t}^{-1}(x))|\leq P_{10}(M_{1,F},M_{2,F},1/m_{1,F})\exp[P_{10}(M_{1,F},M_{2,F},1/m_{1,F})]\;.

(246)

For any
x\in F^{-1}(0)
and
t\in\operatorname{B}_{\infty}(0,\eta)
we have

\mathrm{D}_{t}\varphi_{\Psi}(\bar{\Phi}_{t}^{-1}(x))=\mathrm{D}\varphi_{\Psi}(\bar{\Phi}_{t}^{-1}(x))\mathrm{D}_{t}\bar{\Phi}_{t}^{-1}(x)\;.

(247)

Combining this result and (
239
), there exists

P_{11}\in\mathtt{Poly}(3,\mathbb{R}_{+})
such that for any
x\in F^{-1}(0)
and

t\in\operatorname{B}_{\infty}(0,\eta)
we have

\|\mathrm{D}_{t}\varphi(\bar{\Phi}_{t}^{-1}(x))\|\leq M_{1,\varphi_{\Psi}}P_{11}(,M_{1,F},M_{2,F},1/m_{1,F})\exp[P_{11}(,M_{1,F},M_{2,F},1/m_{1,F})]\;.

(248)

For any
x\in F^{-1}(0)
,
t\in\operatorname{B}_{\infty}(0,\eta)
we have

\mathrm{D}_{t}\mathrm{J}F(\bar{\Phi}_{t}^{-1}(x))^{-1}=\mathrm{D}_{t}\det(G(\bar{\Phi}_{t}^{-1}(x)))^{1/2}=\mathrm{J}F(\bar{\Phi}_{t}^{-1}(x))^{-1}\mathrm{D}_{t}\det(G(\bar{\Phi}_{t}^{-1}(x))^{-1})\;.

(249)

In addition, for any
x\in F^{-1}(0)
,
t\in\operatorname{B}_{\infty}(0,\eta)
and

h\in\mathbb{R}^{p}
we have

\mathrm{D}_{t}\det(G(\bar{\Phi}_{t}^{-1}(x)))(h)=\operatorname{Tr}(\mathrm{Adj}(G(\bar{\Phi}_{t}^{-1}(x)))\mathrm{D}G(\bar{\Phi}_{t}^{-1}(x))(\mathrm{D}_{t}\bar{\Phi}_{t}^{-1}(x)(h)))\;.

(250)

Using this result, (
249
), (
200
) and
(
239
), there exists

P_{12}\in\mathtt{Poly}(3,\mathbb{R}_{+})
such that for any
x\in F^{-1}(0)
,

t\in\operatorname{B}_{\infty}(0,\eta)
we have

\|\mathrm{D}_{t}\mathrm{J}F(\bar{\Phi}_{t}^{-1}(x))^{-1}\|\leq P_{12}(M_{1,F},M_{2,F},1/m_{1,F})\exp[P_{12}(M_{1,F},M_{2,F},1/m_{1,F})]\;.

(251)

Finally, for any
x\in F^{-1}(0)
,
t\in\operatorname{B}_{\infty}(0,\eta)
and
h\in\mathbb{R}^{p}
we have

\mathrm{D}_{t}\det(\mathrm{D}\bar{\Phi}_{t}^{-1}(x))=\operatorname{Tr}(\mathrm{Adj}(\mathrm{D}\bar{\Phi}_{t}^{-1}(x))\mathrm{D}_{t}\mathrm{D}\bar{\Phi}_{t}^{-1}(x)(h))\;.

(252)

Hence, using (
239
), there exists

P_{13}\in\mathtt{Poly}(4,\mathbb{R}_{+})
such that for any
x\in F^{-1}(0)
and

t\in\operatorname{B}_{\infty}(0,\eta)
we have

\displaystyle\|\mathrm{D}_{t}\det(\mathrm{D}\bar{\Phi}_{t}^{-1}(x))\|

\displaystyle\leq P_{13}(M_{1,F},M_{2,F},M_{3,F},1/m_{1,F})

(253)

\displaystyle\qquad\times\exp[P_{13}(M_{1,F},M_{2,F},M_{3,F},1/m_{1,F})]\;.

(254)

We conclude the proof upon combining (
242
),
(
245
), (
246
),
(
248
), (
251
) and
(
253
).
∎

A.4
Regularity results

In the following section we prove a smoothing lemma which is key to extend

Section

4.1.3
to the case where
\Psi
and
\varphi
are no longer
in
\mathrm{C}^{1}(\bar{\mathsf{U}},\mathbb{R})
but are Lipschitz continuous.

Lemma A.6
.

Let
\mathsf{U}
be open bounded,
r>0
,

\mathsf{V}=\mathsf{U}+\operatorname{B}(0,r)
and
\varphi\in\mathrm{C}(\mathbb{R}^{d},\mathbb{R})
which
satisfies (
19
) and is Lipschitz on
\mathsf{V}
, i.e. there
exists
M_{1,\varphi}\geq 0
such that for any
x,y\in\mathsf{V}
we have

\left|\varphi(x)-\varphi(y)\right|\leq M_{1,\varphi}\left\|x-y\right\|\;.

(255)

In addition, let

M_{0,\varphi}=\sup\{|\varphi(x)|\,:\;x\in\mathsf{V}\}
.
Then there exist
\bar{\delta}>0
and

\{\varphi^{\delta}\,:\;\delta\in(0,\bar{\delta})\}
such
that the following hold:

(a)

For any
x\in\mathsf{U}
,

\lim_{\delta\to 0}\varphi^{\delta}(x)=\varphi(x)
.

(b)

For any
\delta\in(0,\bar{\delta})
,

\varphi^{\delta}\in\mathrm{C}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d},\mathbb{R})
and there exists

\mathtt{L}_{\delta}\geq 0
such that for any
x,y\in\mathsf{U}
,

\left|\varphi^{\delta}(x)-\varphi^{\delta}(y)\right|\leq\mathtt{L}_{\delta}\left\|x-y\right\|\;.

(256)

Let

\mathtt{L}_{0}=\sup\{\mathtt{L}_{\delta}\,:\;\delta\in(0,\bar{\delta})\}<+\infty
and we have

\mathtt{L}_{0}\leq C_{1}(1+M_{0,\varphi}+M_{1,\varphi})(1+C_{\varphi})
, with

C_{1}\geq 0
independent from
\varphi
.

(c)

For any
\delta\in(0,\bar{\delta})
there
exists
M_{\delta}\geq 0
such that for any
x\in\mathsf{U}
,

|\varphi^{\delta}(x)|\leq M_{\delta}
and

M_{0}=\sup\{M_{\delta}\,:\;\delta\in(0,\bar{\delta})\}<+\infty
. In addition,
M_{0}\leq C_{2}(1+M_{0,\varphi})(1+C_{\varphi})
with

C_{2}\geq 0
independent from
\varphi
.

(d)

For any
\delta\in(0,\bar{\delta})
there
exists
D_{\delta}\geq 0
such that for any
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
we have

|\varphi^{\delta}(x)|\leq D_{\delta}\exp[D_{\delta}\left\|x\right\|^{\upalpha k}]\;,

(257)

and
D_{\delta}\leq C_{3}(1+C_{\varphi})\exp[C_{3}C_{\varphi}]
with
C_{3}
independent from
\varphi
.

(e)

Assume
1
, that
F^{-1}(0)\subset\mathsf{U}

and that there exists
m_{0,\varphi}>0
such that for any

x\in F^{-1}(0)
,
\varphi(x)\geq m_{0,\varphi}
. Then there exists
\bar{\delta}^{\star}>0
such that for any

\delta\in(0,\bar{\delta}^{\star})
and
x\in F^{-1}(0)
,

\varphi^{\delta}(x)\geq m_{0,\varphi}/2
. In addition if
\varphi\geq 0

then for any
\delta>0
,
\varphi^{\delta}\geq 0
.

In addition,
\bar{\delta}=f_{1}(C_{\varphi})
and

\bar{\delta}^{\star}=f_{2}(M_{0,\varphi},M_{1,\varphi},1/m_{0,\varphi},C_{\varphi})
with
f_{1}\in\mathrm{C}(\mathbb{R}_{+},\mathbb{R}_{+})
and

f_{2}\in\mathrm{C}(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{4},\mathbb{R}_{+})
and are non-decreasing w.r.t to each of their
variables. Finally, there exists
C_{4}\geq 0
such that

\limsup_{\delta\to 0}M_{\delta}\leq C_{4}M_{0,\varphi}\;,\quad\limsup_{\delta\to 0}\mathtt{L}_{\delta}\leq C_{4}M_{1,\varphi}\;,\quad\limsup_{\delta\to 0}D_{\delta}\leq C_{4}C_{\varphi}\;,

(258)

with
C_{4}
independent from
\varphi
.

Proof.

Since
\mathsf{U}
is bounded there exists
R\geq 0
such that

\mathsf{U}\subset\bar{\operatorname{B}}(0,R)
. Let
p\in\mathbb{N}^{\star}
such that
2p>\upalpha k

and for any
\delta>0
define
k_{\delta}:\ \mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}_{+}
such that
for any
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
we have

\textstyle{k_{\delta}(x)=\exp[-\left\|x\right\|^{2p}/\delta]/\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\exp[-\left\|\tilde{x}\right\|^{2p}/\delta]\mathrm{d}\tilde{x}\;.}

(259)

Let
C_{1}=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\exp[-\left\|\tilde{x}\right\|^{2p}]\mathrm{d}\tilde{x}
and we
have for any
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
,

k_{\delta}(x)=C_{1}^{-1}\delta^{-d/2p}\exp[-\left\|x\right\|^{2p}/\delta]
. Note
that since
2p>\upalpha k
and using (
19
) we have that for
any
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}|\varphi(x-y)|k_{\delta}(y)\mathrm{d}y<+\infty
. For
any
\delta>0
we define
\varphi^{\delta}
such that for any
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}

\textstyle{\varphi^{\delta}(x)=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\varphi(x-y)k_{\delta}(y)\mathrm{d}y\;.}

(260)

We divide the rest of the proof into five parts.

(a)

We have that for any
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
,

\lim_{\delta\to 0}\varphi^{\delta}(x)=\varphi(x)
, since

\varphi\in\mathrm{C}(\mathbb{R}^{d},\mathbb{R})
and

\{k_{\delta}\,:\;\delta>0\}
is a mollifier. This concludes
the proof of
Lemma

A.6
-
(a)
.

(b)

Using that
2p>\upalpha k
and (
19
) we have that
for any
\upbeta\in\mathbb{N}
,

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left\|y\right\|^{\upbeta}|\varphi(x-y)|k_{\delta}(y)<+\infty
. Hence,
\varphi^{\delta}\in\mathrm{C}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d},\mathbb{R})
. Let

x\in\mathsf{U}
. Using that

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}y\|y\|^{2p-1}k_{\delta}(y)\mathrm{d}y=0
, we have
that for any
\delta>0

\displaystyle\textstyle{\|\nabla\varphi^{\delta}(x)\|=(2p/\delta)\|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\varphi(x-y)y\|y\|^{2p-2}k_{\delta}(y)\mathrm{d}y\|}

(261)

\displaystyle\quad\textstyle{=(2p/\delta)\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}|\varphi(x-y)-\varphi(x)|\|y\|^{2p-1}k_{\delta}(y)\mathrm{d}y}

(262)

\displaystyle\quad\textstyle{\leq(2pM_{1,\varphi}/\delta)\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\|y\|^{2p}k_{\delta}(y)\mathrm{d}y+(2p/\delta)\int_{\bar{\operatorname{B}}(0,r/2)^{\mathrm{c}}}|\varphi(x-y)-\varphi(x)|\|y\|^{2p-1}k_{\delta}(y)\mathrm{d}y}

(263)

\displaystyle\quad\textstyle{\leq(2pM_{1,\varphi}/C_{1})\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\|y\|^{2p}\exp[-\|y\|^{2p}]\mathrm{d}y+(2p/\delta)\int_{\bar{\operatorname{B}}(0,r/2)^{\mathrm{c}}}|\varphi(x-y)-\varphi(x)|\|y\|^{2p-1}k_{\delta}(y)\mathrm{d}y\;.}

(264)

We now bound the second term. Let

\bar{\upalpha}_{k}=\lceil\upalpha k\rceil
. Using (
19
),
we have for any and
x\in\mathsf{U}
and
y\in\mathbb{R}^{d}

\displaystyle|\varphi(x-y)-\varphi(x)|

\displaystyle\leq(1+M_{0,\varphi})C_{\varphi}\exp[C_{\varphi}\left\|x-y\right\|^{\upalpha k}]

(265)

\displaystyle\leq(1+M_{0,\varphi})C_{\varphi}\exp[3^{\bar{\upalpha}_{k}-1}C_{\varphi}(1+\|x\|^{\bar{\upalpha}_{k}}+\|y\|^{\bar{\upalpha}_{k}})]

(266)

\displaystyle\leq(1+M_{0,\varphi})C_{\varphi}\exp[3^{\bar{\upalpha}_{k}-1}C_{\varphi}(1+R^{\bar{\upalpha}_{k}})]\exp[3^{\bar{\upalpha}_{k}-1}C_{\varphi}\|y\|^{\bar{\upalpha}_{k}}]\;.

(267)

Therefore we get that for any
x\in\mathsf{U}
and
y\in\bar{\operatorname{B}}(0,r/2)^{\mathrm{c}}

|\varphi(x-y)-\varphi(x)|\left\|y\right\|^{2p-1}\leq(1+M_{0,\varphi})C_{\varphi}\exp[3^{\bar{\upalpha}_{k}-1}C_{\varphi}(1+R^{\bar{\upalpha}_{k}})]\exp[(3^{\bar{\upalpha}_{k}}(r/2)^{\bar{\upalpha}_{k}-2p}C_{\varphi}+1)\left\|y\right\|^{2p}]\;.

(268)

Therefore there exists
C_{a}\geq 0
such that for any
x\in\mathsf{U}
and

y\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
we have

|\varphi(x-y)-\varphi(x)|\left\|y\right\|^{2p-1}\leq C_{a}(1+M_{0,\varphi})C_{\varphi}\exp[C_{a}(1+C_{\varphi})\left\|y\right\|^{2p}]\;,

(269)

with
C_{a}\geq 0
independent from
\varphi
. Using this result, we have
for any
x\in\mathsf{U}
and
\delta\in\left(0,1/(2C_{a}(1+C_{\varphi}))\right)

\displaystyle\textstyle{(2p/\delta)\int_{\bar{\operatorname{B}}(0,r/2)^{\mathrm{c}}}|\varphi(x-y)-\varphi(x)|\left\|y\right\|^{2p-1}k_{\delta}(y)\mathrm{d}y}

(270)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\textstyle{\leq 2pC_{a}(1+M_{0,\varphi})C_{\varphi}\delta^{-d/2p-1}C_{1}^{-1}\int_{\bar{\operatorname{B}}(0,r/2)^{\mathrm{c}}}\exp[(C_{a}(1+C_{\varphi})-1/\delta)\left\|y\right\|^{2p}]\mathrm{d}y}

(271)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\textstyle{\leq 2^{1+d(1+2p)/2p}pC_{a}(1+M_{0,\varphi})C_{\varphi}/(\delta C_{1})\int_{\bar{\operatorname{B}}(0,r/(2^{1+2p}\delta)^{1/2p})^{\mathrm{c}}}\exp[-\left\|y\right\|^{2p}]\mathrm{d}y\;.}

(272)

We have for any
\delta\in\left(0,1/(2C_{a}(1+C_{\varphi}))\right)

\textstyle{\int_{\bar{\operatorname{B}}(0,r/(2^{1+2p}\delta)^{1/2p})^{\mathrm{c}}}\exp[-\left\|y\right\|^{2p}]\mathrm{d}y/C_{1}\leq\exp[-r^{2p}/(2^{2+2p}\delta)]\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\exp[-\left\|y\right\|^{2p}/2]\mathrm{d}y/C_{1}\;.}

(273)

Therefore, there exists
C_{b}\geq 0
(independent from
\varphi
) such that for any

x\in\mathsf{U}
and
\delta\in(0,1/(2C_{a}(1+C_{\varphi})))
we have

\textstyle{(2p/\delta)\int_{\bar{\operatorname{B}}(0,r/2)^{\mathrm{c}}}|\varphi(x-y)-\varphi(x)|\left\|y\right\|^{2p-1}k_{\delta}(y)\mathrm{d}y\leq C_{b}(1+M_{0,\varphi})C_{\varphi}\delta^{-1}\exp[-r^{2p}/(2^{2+2p}\delta)]\;.}

(274)

Combining this bound and (
264
), we get that for any

\delta\in(0,1/(2C_{a}(1+C_{\varphi})))
there exists

\mathtt{L}_{\delta}\geq 0
such that for any
x\in\mathsf{U}
,

\|\nabla\varphi^{\delta}(x)\|\leq\mathtt{L}_{\delta}
. Let

\mathtt{L}_{0}=\sup\{\mathtt{L}_{\delta}\,:\;\delta\in(0,\bar{\delta})\}<+\infty
and using, (
264
), (
274
) and
that for any
t\geq 0
,
t\exp[-r^{2p}t/2^{2+2p}]\leq 2^{2+2p}/(\mathrm{e}r^{2p})
we have

\textstyle{\mathtt{L}_{0}\leq(2pM_{1,\varphi}/C_{1})\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\|y\|^{2p}\exp[-\|y\|^{2p}]\mathrm{d}y+2^{2+2p}C_{b}(1+M_{0,\varphi})C_{\varphi}/(\mathrm{e}r^{2p})\;.}

(275)

This concludes the proof of
Lemma

A.6
-
(b)
.

(c)

For any
x\in\mathsf{U}
and
\delta\in(1/(2C_{a}(1+C_{\varphi})))
we have

\displaystyle|\varphi(x)|

\displaystyle\textstyle{\leq\int_{\bar{\operatorname{B}}(0,r/2)}|\varphi(y-x)|k_{\delta}(y)\mathrm{d}y+\int_{\bar{\operatorname{B}}(0,r/2)^{\mathrm{c}}}|\varphi(y-x)|k_{\delta}(y)\mathrm{d}y}

(276)

\displaystyle\textstyle{\leq M_{0,\varphi}+\int_{\bar{\operatorname{B}}(0,r/2)^{\mathrm{c}}}|\varphi(y-x)|k_{\delta}(y)\mathrm{d}y\;.}

(277)

Similarly to (
274
), there exists
c\geq 0

(independent from
\varphi
) such that for
x\in\mathsf{U}
and

\delta\in(1/(2C_{a}(1+C_{\varphi})))

\textstyle{\int_{\bar{\operatorname{B}}(0,r/2)^{\mathrm{c}}}|\varphi(y-x)|k_{\delta}(y)\mathrm{d}y\leq(C_{\varphi}/c)\exp[-c/\delta]\;.}

(278)

Combining this result and (
276
) for any

\delta\in\left(0,1/(2C_{a}(1+C_{\varphi}))\right)
there exists
M_{\delta}>0
such
that for any
x\in\mathsf{U}
,
|\varphi^{\delta}(x)|\leq M_{\delta}
. Let

M_{0}=\sup\{M_{\delta}\,:\;\delta\in(0,\bar{\delta})\}<+\infty
. We have that

M_{0}\leq M_{0,\varphi}+(C_{\varphi}/c)\exp[-c(1+C_{\varphi})]\;,

(279)

which concludes the proof of
Lemma

A.6
-
(c)
.

(d)

Using that for any
a,b\geq 0
and
p\geq 0
,

(a+b)^{p}\leq 2^{\min(p-1,0)}(a^{p}+b^{p})
we have that for any

x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
and
\delta>0

\textstyle{|\varphi^{\delta}(x)|\leq C_{\varphi}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\exp[C_{\varphi}\beta_{\upalpha,k}(\left\|x\right\|^{\upalpha k}+\left\|y\right\|^{\upalpha k})]k_{\delta}(y)\mathrm{d}y\;,}

(280)

where
\beta_{\upalpha,k}=2^{\min(\upalpha k-1,0)}
. Hence, using this
result we have for any
\delta\in\left(0,1/(2\beta_{\upalpha,k}C_{\varphi})\right)

\displaystyle|\varphi^{\delta}(x)|

\displaystyle\textstyle{\leq C_{\varphi}/(C_{1}\delta^{d/2p})\exp[C_{\varphi}\beta_{\upalpha,k}\left\|x\right\|^{\upalpha k}]\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\exp[(C_{\varphi}\beta_{\upalpha,k}-1/\delta)\left\|y\right\|^{2p}]\mathrm{d}y}

(281)

\displaystyle\textstyle{\leq C_{\varphi}/(C_{1}\delta^{d/2p})\exp[C_{\varphi}\beta_{\upalpha,k}\left\|x\right\|^{\upalpha k}]\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\exp[-\left\|y\right\|^{2p}/(2\delta)]\mathrm{d}y\;}

(282)

\displaystyle\textstyle{\leq 2^{d/2p}C_{\varphi}\exp[C_{\varphi}\beta_{\upalpha,k}\left\|x\right\|^{\upalpha k}]\;.}

(283)

Therefore, for any
\delta\in\left(0,1/(2\beta_{\upalpha,k}C_{\varphi})\right)

there exists
D_{\delta}\geq 0
such that for any
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
,

\varphi(x)\leq D_{\delta}\exp[D_{\delta}\left\|x\right\|^{\upalpha k}]
. In addition,
there exists
C_{d}\geq 0
(independent of
\varphi
) such that

D_{\delta}\leq C_{d}(1+C_{\varphi})\exp[C_{d}C_{\varphi}]
, which concludes the
proof of
Lemma

A.6
-
(d)
.

(e)

If
\varphi\geq 0
then for any
\delta>0
,
\varphi^{\delta}\geq 0

using (
260
). For any
x\in\mathsf{U}
we have

\textstyle{\varphi^{\delta}(x)=\varphi(x)+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}(\varphi(y-x)-\varphi(x))k_{\delta}(y)\mathrm{d}y\geq m_{0,\varphi}-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left|\varphi(y-x)-\varphi(x)\right|k_{\delta}(y)\;.}

(284)

For any
x\in\mathsf{U}
we have

\textstyle{\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left|\varphi(y-x)-\varphi(x)\right|k_{\delta}(y)\leq M_{1,\varphi}\delta^{1/k}+\int_{\bar{\operatorname{B}}(0,r/2)^{\mathrm{c}}}|\varphi(y-x)-\varphi(x)|k_{\delta}(y)\mathrm{d}y\;.}

(285)

Similarly to (
274
), there exists
C_{d}\geq 0
independent from
\varphi
such that for any
x\in\mathsf{U}

\textstyle{\int_{\bar{\operatorname{B}}(0,r/2)^{\mathrm{c}}}|\varphi(y-x)-\varphi(x)|k_{\delta}(y)\mathrm{d}y\leq C_{d}(1+M_{0,\varphi})C_{\varphi}\exp[-1/(C_{d}\delta)]\;.}

(286)

Let
\bar{\delta}_{1}=(m_{0,\varphi}/(4M_{1,\varphi}))^{k}
and

\bar{\delta}_{2}=C_{d}^{-1}(\log(4C_{d}C_{\varphi}(1+M_{0,\varphi}))-\min(0,\log(m_{0,\varphi})))^{-1}
. Then for any

\delta\in(0,\min(\bar{\delta}_{1},\bar{\delta}_{2}))
we have that for any

x\in\mathsf{U}

\textstyle{\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left|\varphi(y-x)-\varphi(x)\right|k_{\delta}(y)\leq m_{0,\varphi}/2\;,}

(287)

which concludes the proof of
Lemma

A.6
-
(e)
upon
combining this result with (
284
).

∎

Appendix B
Technical results for
Section

4.3.1

In this section, we derive a quantitative parametric theory for Laplace-type
expansion in
Section

B.1
and
Section

B.2
. We
start by deriving technical bounds in
Section

B.1
. Our main
result,
Proposition

B.5
, is presented in

Section

B.2
along with a quantitative Morse lemma. Finally, in

Section

B.3
we derive some moments bounds.

Let
u:\ \mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathsf{Z}\to\mathbb{R}
with
\mathsf{Z}
a topological space. We
consider the following assumption.

H 5
.

u\in\mathrm{C}(\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathsf{Z},\mathbb{R})
, for any
z\in\mathsf{Z}
,

u(\cdot,z)\in\mathrm{C}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d},\mathbb{R})
and the following hold:

(a)

\mathsf{Z}
is compact.

(b)

There exist
N\in\mathbb{N}
,
\{x_{0}^{k}\}_{k=1}^{N}\in\mathrm{C}(\mathsf{Z},\mathbb{R}^{d})

such that

\operatorname*{arg\,min}\{u(x,z)\,:\;x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}\}\subset\{x_{0}^{k}(z)\}_{k=1}^{N}

and
\operatorname*{arg\,min}\{u(x,z)\,:\;x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}\}\neq\emptyset
for any

z\in\mathsf{Z}
.

(c)

There exists
\mathtt{M}\geq 0
such that for any
x_{1},x_{2}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
and

z\in\mathsf{Z}
,

\|\nabla^{k}_{x}u(x_{1},z)-\nabla^{k}_{x}u(x_{2},z)\|\leq\mathtt{M}\|x_{1}-x_{2}\|
.

(d)

There exists
\mathtt{m},\upalpha>0
and
R\geq 0
such that for any
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}

with
\|x\|\geq R
,
u(x,z)\geq\mathtt{m}\|x\|^{\upalpha}
.

We denote
u^{\star}:\ \mathsf{Z}\to\mathbb{R}
such that for any
z\in\mathsf{Z}
,

u^{\star}(z)=\min\{u(x,z)\,:\;x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}\}
. Under

5
we have that for any
z\in\mathsf{Z}
,

u^{\star}=\min\{u(x_{0}^{k}(z),z)\,:\;k\in\{1,\dots,N\}\}
and
therefore
u^{\star}\in\mathrm{C}(\mathsf{Z},\mathbb{R})
. For any

\varphi:\ \mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}_{+}
and
\varepsilon>0
we define

\displaystyle\textstyle{\mathcal{I}_{\varepsilon}(\varphi,z)=C_{\varepsilon}^{-1}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\varphi(x)\exp[-(u(x,z)-u^{\star}(z))/\varepsilon]\mathrm{d}x\;,\quad\mathcal{J}_{\varepsilon}(z)=\mathcal{I}_{\varepsilon}(1,z)\;,}

(288)

\displaystyle\textstyle{C_{\varepsilon}=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\exp[-\left\|x\right\|^{2}/\varepsilon]\mathrm{d}x=(\uppi\varepsilon)^{d/2}\;.}

(289)

In addition, we define

\textstyle{\mathcal{I}_{0}(\varphi,z)=\int_{\operatorname*{arg\,min}u(\cdot,z)}\varphi(x)\det(\nabla^{2}_{x}u(x,z))^{-1/2}\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{0}(x)\;,\quad\mathcal{J}_{0}(z)=\mathcal{I}_{0}(1,z)\;,}

(290)

where
\det(\nabla^{2}_{x}u(x,z))^{-1/2}=+\infty
if
\nabla^{2}_{x}u(x,z)
is not
invertible. If
\varphi:\ \mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}
and

I_{\varepsilon}(\left|\varphi\right|)<+\infty
for some
\varepsilon\geq 0
we define

\mathcal{I}_{\varepsilon}(\varphi)
similarly as in (
288
), (
290
). For
any
\varepsilon\geq 0
and
\varphi:\ \mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}^{p}
such that it is
defined we let

\updelta_{z}\mathrm{S}_{\varepsilon}[\varphi]=\mathcal{I}_{\varepsilon}(\varphi,z)/\mathcal{J}_{\varepsilon}(z)
. We emphasize that these definitions are the parametric
counterparts to the ones introduced in
Section

4.1
.

In what follows, we define
\sigma:\ \mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathsf{Z}\to\left[0,+\infty\right]

such that for any
z\in\mathsf{Z}
and
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}

\sigma(x,z)
is the inverse
of the minimum eigenvalue of
\nabla^{2}_{x}u(x,z)
. In addition, for any

\upbeta>0
, we define
\sigma^{\star}_{\upbeta}:\ \mathsf{Z}\to\left[0,+\infty\right]
such that
for any
z\in\mathsf{Z}
,

\sigma^{\star}_{\upbeta}(z)=\int_{\operatorname*{arg\,min}u(\cdot,z)}\sigma(x,z)^{\upbeta}\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{0}(x)
.

B.1
Parametric lower and truncation bounds

Lemma B.1
.

Assume
5
. Then, for any
\bar{\varepsilon}\geq 0
there exists

A_{0}>0
such that for any
z\in\mathsf{Z}
and
\varepsilon\in\left[0,\bar{\varepsilon}\right]

we have
\mathcal{J}_{\varepsilon}(z)\geq A_{0}
.

Proof.

Let
z\in\mathsf{Z}
and
\varepsilon>0
. Let
x^{\star}(z)
be a global minimizer of

x\mapsto u(x,z)
which exists since

\operatorname*{arg\,min}\{u(x,z)\,:\;x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}\}\neq\emptyset
. Using the change of variable

x\mapsto x+x^{\star}(z)
we have

\textstyle{\mathcal{J}_{\varepsilon}(z)=C_{\varepsilon}^{-1}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\exp[-(u(x,z)-u^{\star}(z))/\varepsilon]\mathrm{d}x=C_{\varepsilon}^{-1}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\exp[-(u(x^{\star}(z)+x,z)-u^{\star}(z))/\varepsilon]\mathrm{d}x\;.}

(291)

For any
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
we have

\textstyle{u(x^{\star}(z)+x,z)-u^{\star}(z)=\int_{0}^{1}\langle\nabla_{x}u(x^{\star}(z)+tx,z)-\nabla_{x}u(x^{\star}(z),z),x\rangle\mathrm{d}t\leq\mathtt{M}\|x\|^{2}/2\;.}

(292)

Combining this result and (
291
) we get

\mathcal{J}_{\varepsilon}(z)\geq\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\exp[-(\mathtt{M}/2)\|x\|^{2}/\varepsilon]\mathrm{d}x/C_{\varepsilon}\geq(2/\mathtt{M})^{d/2}
. In addition, we have that for any

x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
,

\mathcal{J}_{0}(z)=\int_{\operatorname*{arg\,min}u(\cdot,z)}\det(\nabla^{2}_{x}u(x,z))^{-1/2}\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{0}(x)
. Note
that if
\sigma_{1}^{\star}(z)=+\infty
, then there exists

\tilde{x}(z)\in\operatorname*{arg\,min}\{u(x,z)\,:\;x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}\}
such that

\sigma(\tilde{x}(z),z)=+\infty
and therefore
\mathcal{J}_{0}(z)=+\infty
. We have
that for any
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
,
\det(\nabla^{2}_{x}u(x,z))\leq\mathtt{M}^{d}
and
therefore,
\mathcal{J}_{0}(z)\geq N\mathtt{M}^{-d/2}
. We conclude upon letting

A_{0}=\min(N,2^{d/2})/\mathtt{M}^{d/2}
.
∎

Lemma B.2
.

Assume
5
. Let
\varphi:\ \mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}
and

C_{\varphi}\geq 0
such that for any
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}

\left|\varphi(x)\right|\leq C_{\varphi}\exp[C_{\varphi}\left\|x\right\|^{\upalpha}]\;.

(293)

Let
\bar{\varepsilon}\in(0,\mathtt{m}/(1+C_{\varphi}))
and
z\in\mathsf{Z}
.
Assume that there exists
\mathsf{V}(z)\subset\mathbb{R}^{d}
open and bounded such that

\operatorname*{arg\,min}\{u(x,z)\,:\;x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}\}\subset\mathsf{V}(z)\subset\operatorname*{arg\,min}\{u(x,z)\,:\;x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}\}+\bar{\operatorname{B}}(0,1)\;.

(294)

Then, there exist
\beta_{1}>0
and
A_{1}\in\mathrm{C}(\mathbb{R}_{+},\mathbb{R}_{+})
such that
for any
\varepsilon\in\left(0,\bar{\varepsilon}\right)

\mathcal{I}_{\varepsilon}^{\mathrm{out}}(\varphi,z)\leq A_{1}(C_{\varphi})\varepsilon^{-d/2}\{\exp[-m(z)/\varepsilon]+\exp[-\beta_{1}/\varepsilon]\}\;,

(295)

with
m(z)=\inf\{u(x,z)\,:\;x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}\backslash\mathsf{V}(z)\}-u^{\star}(z)
,

\mathcal{I}_{\varepsilon}^{\mathrm{out}}(\varphi,z)=\mathcal{I}_{\varepsilon}(\varphi\mathbbm{1}_{\mathsf{V}(z)^{\mathrm{c}}},z)
and
A_{1},\beta_{1}
independent from
\varphi
,

z
, with
A_{1}
non-decreasing.

Proof.

First, we have that

\mathsf{V}(z)\subset\cup_{k=1}^{N}\{x_{0}^{k}(z)\,:\;z\in\mathsf{Z}\}+\bar{\operatorname{B}}(0,1)\;.

(296)

Since
\mathsf{Z}
is compact and that for any
k\in\{1,\dots,N\}
,

x_{0}^{k}\in\mathrm{C}(\mathsf{Z},\mathbb{R}^{d})
, there exists
R^{\prime}\geq 0
(independent from

z
) such that
R^{\prime}\geq R
(where
R
is given in
5
) and

\mathsf{V}(z)\subset\bar{\operatorname{B}}(0,R^{\prime})
. Note that for any
\varepsilon>0
, we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{\varepsilon}^{\mathrm{out}}(\varphi,z)=\mathcal{I}_{\varepsilon}^{1}(\varphi,z)+\mathcal{I}_{\varepsilon}^{2}(\varphi,z)\;,

(297)

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{\varepsilon}^{1}(\varphi,z)=\mathcal{I}_{\varepsilon}(\varphi\mathbbm{1}_{\bar{\operatorname{B}}(0,R^{\prime})^{\mathrm{c}}},z)\;,\quad\mathcal{I}_{\varepsilon}^{2}(\varphi,z)=\mathcal{I}_{\varepsilon}(\varphi\mathbbm{1}_{\mathsf{V}^{\mathrm{c}}\cap\bar{\operatorname{B}}(0,R^{\prime})},z)\;.

(298)

Let
\varepsilon\in\left(0,\bar{\varepsilon}\right)
. The rest of the proof is similar to the
one of
Lemma

A.3
but is given for completeness. We divide
the proof into two parts. First, we bound
\mathcal{I}_{\varepsilon}^{1}(\varphi,z)
and
then
\mathcal{I}_{\varepsilon}^{2}(\varphi,z)
.

(a)

Let
w=(\mathtt{m}/\varepsilon-C_{\varphi})^{1/\upalpha}
(which makes
sense, since
\varepsilon<\mathtt{m}/(C_{\varphi}+1)
). Since
R^{\prime}\geq R
, using (
293
) and the fact that
u\geq 1
, we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{\varepsilon}^{1}(\varphi,z)

\displaystyle\textstyle{=C_{1}^{-1}\varepsilon^{-d/2}\int_{\bar{\operatorname{B}}(0,R^{\prime})^{\mathrm{c}}}\varphi(x)\exp[-(u(x,z)-u^{\star}(z))/\varepsilon]\mathrm{d}x}

(299)

\displaystyle\textstyle{\leq C_{1}^{-1}C_{\varphi}\varepsilon^{-d/2}\exp[\bar{u}^{\star}]\int_{\bar{\operatorname{B}}(0,R^{\prime})^{\mathrm{c}}}\exp[-(\mathtt{m}/\varepsilon-C_{\varphi})\left\|x\right\|^{\upalpha}]\mathrm{d}x}

(300)

\displaystyle\leq\textstyle{C_{1}^{-1}C_{\varphi}\exp[\bar{u}^{\star}]\varepsilon^{-d/2}\int_{\bar{\operatorname{B}}(0,R^{\prime}w)^{\mathrm{c}}}\exp[-\left\|x\right\|^{\upalpha}]\mathrm{d}x\;,}

(301)

where
\bar{u}^{\star}=\sup\{u^{\star}(z)\,:\;z\in\mathsf{Z}\}
. Let

C_{\upalpha}=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\exp[-\left\|x\right\|^{\upalpha}]\mathrm{d}x
. Using that

w=(\mathtt{m}/\varepsilon-C_{\varphi})^{1/\upalpha}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{\varepsilon}^{1}(\varphi,z)

\displaystyle\leq\textstyle{C_{1}^{-1}C_{\varphi}\exp[\bar{u}^{\star}]\varepsilon^{-d/2}\int_{\bar{\operatorname{B}}(0,R^{\prime}w)^{\mathrm{c}}}\exp[-\left\|x\right\|^{\upalpha}]\mathrm{d}x}

(302)

\displaystyle\leq\textstyle{C_{1}^{-1}C_{\varphi}\exp[\bar{u}^{\star}]\varepsilon^{-d/2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\exp[-\left\|x\right\|^{\upalpha}/2]\mathrm{d}x\exp[-(R^{\prime}u)^{\upalpha}/2]}

(303)

\displaystyle\leq\textstyle{2^{d/\upalpha}C_{\upalpha}C_{1}^{-1}C_{\varphi}\exp[\bar{u}^{\star}]\exp[(R^{\prime})^{\upalpha}C_{\varphi}/2]\varepsilon^{-d/2}\exp[-(R^{\prime})^{\upalpha}\mathtt{m}/(2\varepsilon)]\leq A_{1}^{1}\varepsilon^{-d/2}\exp[-\beta_{1}^{1}/\varepsilon]\;,}

(304)

with

A_{1}^{1}=2^{d/\upalpha}C_{\upalpha}C_{1}^{-1}C_{\varphi}\exp[\bar{u}^{\star}]\exp[(R^{\prime})^{\upalpha}C_{\varphi}/2]\;,\qquad\beta_{1}^{1}=(R^{\prime})^{\upalpha}\mathtt{m}/2\;.

(305)

(b)

Second, let
\mathsf{K}(z)=\mathsf{V}^{\mathrm{c}}(z)\cap\bar{\operatorname{B}}(0,R^{\prime})
. Note
that for any
x\in\mathsf{K}(z)
,
u(x,z)-u^{\star}(z)\geq m(z)
. Hence, we
have

\mathcal{I}_{\varepsilon}^{2}(\varphi,z)\leq C_{1}^{-1}C_{\varphi}\varepsilon^{-d/2}\exp[C_{\varphi}(R^{\prime})^{\upalpha}]\exp[-m(z)/\varepsilon]\lambda(\mathsf{K}(z))\;,

(306)

where we recall that
\lambda(\mathsf{K}(z))
is the Lebesgue measure of
\mathsf{K}(z)
.
Since
\mathsf{K}(z)\subset\bar{\operatorname{B}}(0,R^{\prime})
we have

\mathcal{I}_{\varepsilon}^{2}(\varphi,z)\leq\uppi^{d/2}(R^{\prime})^{d}\Gamma^{-1}(d/2+1)C_{1}^{-1}C_{\varphi}\exp[(R^{\prime})^{\upalpha}]\varepsilon^{-d/2}\exp[-m(z)/\varepsilon]\leq A_{1}^{2}\varepsilon^{-d/2}\exp[-m(z)/\varepsilon]\;,

(307)

where
\Gamma:\ \left(0,+\infty\right)\to\mathbb{R}_{+}
is given for any

s\in\left(0,+\infty\right)
by

\Gamma(s)=\int_{0}^{+\infty}t^{s-1}\exp[-t]\mathrm{d}t
and

A_{1}^{2}=\uppi^{d/2}(R^{\prime})^{d}\Gamma^{-1}(d/2+1)C_{1}^{-1}C_{\varphi}\exp[C_{\varphi}(R^{\prime})^{\upalpha}]\;.

(308)

We conclude the proof upon combining (
304
),
(
307
) and letting
\beta_{1}=\beta_{1}^{1}
and

A_{1}=A_{1}^{1}+A_{1}^{2}
.
∎

B.2
Quantitative Morse lemma and parametric Laplace-type results

We begin by recalling a quantitative version of the Morse lemma, see

[
51
, Theorem 4.2]
.

Proposition B.3
.

Let
\mathsf{U}\subset\mathbb{R}^{d}
be open with
x_{0}\in\mathsf{U}
and

f\in\mathrm{C}^{k}(\mathsf{U},\mathbb{R})
with
k\in\mathbb{N}
and
k\geq 3
. Assume that

\nabla f(x_{0})=0
and let
K\geq 0
such that

K\geq\max_{j\in\{1,\dots,k\}}\sup\{\|\mathrm{D}^{j}f(x)\|\,:\;x\in\mathsf{U}\}<+\infty
. Let
\sigma
be
the minimal eigenvalue of
\nabla^{2}f(x_{0})
and assume that
\sigma>0
. Let

\sigma_{0}=\min(\sigma,1)
. Then, there exist
c_{0}>0
,

\delta=c_{0}\sigma_{0}^{4}
and

\Phi:\ \operatorname{B}(0,\delta)\to\Phi(\operatorname{B}(0,\delta))
such that the following
hold:

(a)

\Phi\in\mathrm{C}^{k-1}(\operatorname{B}(0,\delta),\Phi(\operatorname{B}(0,\delta)))
is a diffeomorphism.

(b)

For any
x\in\operatorname{B}(0,\delta)
,
f(\Phi(x))=f(x_{0})+\|x\|^{2}
.

(c)

There exist
c_{1},\upbeta>0
such that
\max_{j\in\{1,\dots,k-1\}}\sup\{\|\mathrm{D}^{j}\Phi(x)\|\,:\;x\in\operatorname{B}(0,\delta)\}\leq c_{1}\sigma_{0}^{-\upbeta}
.

In addition,
c_{0},c_{1}
and
\upbeta
depend only on
k
,
d
and
K
.

Note that in
[
51
, Theorem 4.2]
, the constant

c_{1}\sigma_{0}^{-\upbeta}
is replaced by
M(K,\sigma_{0},k)
where

M:\ \mathbb{R}_{+}^{3}\to\mathbb{R}_{+}
. However, a close examination of the proof reveals
that the dependency of
M(K,\sigma_{0},k)
with respect to
\sigma_{0}^{-1}
is of
order
\sigma_{0}^{-\upbeta}
for some
\upbeta>0
which can be made
explicit. Using
Proposition

B.3
we derive the following
parametric version of
Section

4.1.2
.

This proposition relies on the notion of
thermodynamic barrier
associated with
u

which we define as follows. Assume that

\operatorname*{arg\,min}\{u(x,z)\,:\;x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}\}\neq\emptyset
for any

z\in\mathsf{Z}
. For any
z\in\mathsf{Z}
we introduce
\mathsf{A}(z)
such that

\mathsf{A}(z)=\emptyset
if there are no other minimizers than the global
minimizers and
\mathsf{A}(z)=\{u(x,z)\,:\;\text{$x$ is a local minimizer of $u(\cdot,z)$
but not a global minimizer}\}
otherwise. Then we define

c^{\star}:\ \mathsf{Z}\to\mathbb{R}
such that for any
z\in\mathsf{Z}
we have

c^{\star}(z)=\inf\mathsf{A}(z)-\inf\{u(x,z)\,:\;x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}\}\;,

(309)

with the convention that
\inf\emptyset=+\infty
. In words, the
thermodynamic barrier
constant quantifies how close the value of the local minimizers are from the
global ones. We refer to
Section

3.2.3
for a discussion on the
importance of
thermodynamic barrier
when establishing parametric Laplace-type expansions.

Proposition B.4
.

Assume
5
. Let
\varphi\in\mathrm{C}(\mathbb{R}^{d},\mathbb{R})
and

z\in\mathsf{Z}
, and assume that
\sigma_{1}^{\star}(z)<+\infty
. There exists
\mathsf{V}(z)
open such that

\operatorname*{arg\,min}\{u(x,z)\,:\;x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}\}\subset\mathsf{V}(z)\subset\operatorname*{arg\,min}\{u(x,z)\,:\;x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}\}+\bar{\operatorname{B}}(0,1)\;.

(310)

We have that

\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}|\mathcal{I}_{\varepsilon}^{\mathrm{in}}(\varphi,z)-\mathcal{I}_{0}(\varphi,z)|=0
, with

\mathcal{I}_{\varepsilon}^{\mathrm{in}}(\varphi,z)=\mathcal{I}_{\varepsilon}(\varphi\mathbbm{1}_{\mathsf{V}(z)},z)
.
Assume that
\varphi\in\mathrm{C}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d},\mathbb{R})
. Then there exist

B_{1}\geq 0
and
\upbeta>0
such that for any
\varepsilon>0
we have

|\mathcal{I}_{\varepsilon}^{\mathrm{in}}(\varphi,z)-\mathcal{I}_{0}(\varphi,z)|\leq B_{1}\sigma_{\upbeta}^{\star}(z)(1+M_{0,\varphi}(z)+M_{1,\varphi}(z))\varepsilon^{1/2}\;,

(311)

with
B_{1}
independent from
\varphi
and
z
and for any
i\in\{0,1\}
,

M_{i,\varphi}(z)=\sup\{\|\nabla^{i}\varphi(x)\|\,:\;x\in\mathsf{V}(z)\}
.
In addition, there exist
c_{1},\upgamma>0
(independent from
z
) such that

m(z)\geq\min(c_{1}/\sigma_{\upgamma}^{\star}(z),c^{\star}(z))
, with

m(z)=\inf\{u(x,z)\,:\;x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}\backslash\mathsf{V}(z)\}-u^{\star}(z)
.

Proof.

Let

\{x_{\star}^{k}(z)\}_{k=1}^{M}=\operatorname*{arg\,min}\{u(x,z)\,:\;x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}\}
. Note that for any
k\in\{1,\dots,M\}
there exists

i_{k}\in\{1,\dots,N\}
such that
x_{\star}^{k}(z)=x_{0}^{i_{k}}(z)
. We recall
that
\mathsf{K}=\bigcup_{k=1}^{N}\{x_{0}^{k}(z)\,:\;z\in\mathsf{Z}\}
is
compact. Therefore there exists
R\geq 0
such that for any
z\in\mathsf{Z}
,

\{x_{\star}^{k}(z)\}_{k=1}^{M}\subset\bar{\operatorname{B}}(0,R)
. We define

K_{\mathrm{global}}\geq 0
such that

K_{\mathrm{global}}=\sup\{\|\nabla_{x}^{j}u(x,z)\|\,:\;x\in\mathsf{K},\ z\in\mathsf{Z},\ j\in\{1,2,3\}\}\;.

(312)

Let
i\in\{1,\dots,M\}
. Using that
\sigma_{1}^{\star}(z)<+\infty
and

Proposition

B.3
with
K\leftarrow K_{\mathrm{global}}

and
k=3
, there exist

\Phi_{i}^{z}\in\mathrm{C}^{2}(\operatorname{B}(0,\delta_{i}(z)),\Phi_{i}^{z}(\operatorname{B}(0,\delta_{i}(z))))

which is a diffeomorphism, with
\Phi_{i}^{z}(0)=x_{\star}^{i}(z)
,

\delta_{i}(z)=c_{0}\min(\sigma^{4}(x_{\star}^{i}(z),z),1)
and for any

x\in\operatorname{B}(0,\delta_{i}(z))
we have

u(\Phi_{i}^{z}(x),z)=u^{\star}+\|x\|^{2}
.

We let

\delta_{0}(z)=\min\{\delta_{i}(z)\,:\;i\in\{1,\dots,M\}\}
.
Using
Proposition

B.3
, there exist
c_{0}^{\prime}\geq 0
and

\upalpha>0
independent from
z
such that for any

\ell\in\{1,\dots,M\}
and
x\in\operatorname{B}(0,\delta_{0}(z))
,

\left\|\mathrm{d}\Phi_{\ell}^{z}(x)\right\|\leq c_{0}^{\prime}\sigma_{\upalpha}^{\star}(z)
. Let

\delta(z)=\min(\delta_{0}(z),1/(c_{0}^{\prime}\sigma_{\upalpha}^{\star}(z)))
. We have
that for any
\ell\in\{1,\dots,N\}
,

\Phi_{\ell}(\operatorname{B}(0,\delta(z)))\subset\operatorname{B}(x_{\star}^{\ell}(z),c_{0}^{\prime}\sigma_{\upalpha}^{\star}(z)\delta(z))\subset\operatorname*{arg\,min}\{u(x,z)\,:\;x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}\}+\bar{\operatorname{B}}(0,1)
. We let

\mathsf{V}(z)=\cup_{i=1}^{M}\Phi_{\ell}(\operatorname{B}(0,\delta(z)))
.

We now show that for any
i,j\in\{1,\dots,M\}
,

\Phi_{i}^{z}(\operatorname{B}(0,\delta(z)))\cap\Phi_{j}^{z}(\operatorname{B}(0,\delta(z)))=\emptyset
. Let
i,j\in\{1,\dots,M\}
and for ease of notation let

\mathsf{W}_{\ell}=\Phi_{\ell}^{z}(\operatorname{B}(0,\delta(z)))
for any

\ell\in\{1,\dots,M\}
. Assume that
\mathsf{W}_{i}\cap\mathsf{W}_{j}\neq\emptyset
. Then, since
\mathsf{W}_{i}
and
\mathsf{W}_{j}
are connected,

\mathsf{W}=\mathsf{W}_{i}\cup\mathsf{W}_{j}
is connected as well. In addition, note that

\Phi_{i}^{z}(0)\notin\mathsf{W}_{j}
and
\Phi_{j}^{z}(0)\notin\mathsf{W}_{i}
. There exists

\gamma\in\mathrm{C}(\left[0,1\right],\mathsf{W})
such that
\gamma(0)=\Phi_{i}^{z}(0)
and

\gamma(1)=\Phi_{j}^{z}(0)
. Denote

t^{\star}=\inf\{t\in\left[0,1\right]\,:\;\gamma(t)\in\mathsf{W}_{j}\}
. We
have that
\gamma(t^{\star})\in\bar{\mathsf{W}}_{j}\backslash\mathsf{W}_{j}
. Hence

u(\gamma(t^{\star}),z)=\delta(z)^{2}+u^{\star}(z)
. But

\gamma(t^{\star})\in\mathsf{W}_{i}
and therefore

u(\gamma(t^{\star}),z)<\delta(z)^{2}+u^{\star}(z)
. This is absurd hence for
any
i,j\in\{1,\dots,M\}
,
\mathsf{W}_{i}\cap\mathsf{W}_{j}=\emptyset
.

Let
\varepsilon>0
and
\mathcal{I}_{0,\varepsilon}^{\mathrm{in}}
be given by

\textstyle{\mathcal{I}_{0,\varepsilon}^{\mathrm{in}}(\varphi,z)=\sum_{\ell=1}^{M}\int_{\operatorname{B}(0,\delta(z)/\varepsilon^{1/2})}\exp[-\left\|x\right\|^{2}]\mathrm{d}x\varphi(x_{\star}^{\ell}(z))\det(\nabla^{2}_{x}u(x_{\star}^{\ell}(z),z))^{-1/2}/C_{1}\;.}

(313)

Recall that
\mathcal{I}_{0}(\varphi,z)=\sum_{\ell=1}^{M}\varphi(x_{\star}^{\ell}(z))\det(\nabla^{2}_{x}u(x_{\star}^{\ell}(z),z))^{-1/2}
.
Therefore, we have

\displaystyle\textstyle{\left|\mathcal{I}_{0}(\varphi,z)-\mathcal{I}_{0,\varepsilon}^{\mathrm{in}}(\varphi,z)\right|}

\displaystyle\leq\textstyle{\sigma_{1/2}^{\star}M_{0,\varphi}\exp[-\delta(z)^{2}/(2\varepsilon)]\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\exp[-\|x\|^{2}/2]\mathrm{d}x/C_{1}}

(314)

\displaystyle\leq 2^{d/2}\sigma_{1/2}^{\star}M_{0,\varphi}\exp[-\delta(z)^{2}/(2\varepsilon)]\;.

(315)

Finally, using (
313
), that for any

\ell\in\{1,\dots,M\}
and
x\in\operatorname{B}(0,\delta(z))
,

u(\Phi_{\ell}^{z}(x),z)=u^{\star}(z)+\|x\|^{2}
, that for any

\ell\in\{1,\dots,M\}
,

\det(\mathrm{d}\Phi_{\ell}^{z}(0))=\det(\nabla^{2}_{x}u(x_{\star}^{\ell}(z),z))^{-1/2}
and

\Phi_{\ell}^{z}(0)=x_{\star}^{\ell}(z)
, we have

\displaystyle\textstyle{|\mathcal{I}_{0,\varepsilon}^{\mathrm{in}}(\varphi,z)-\mathcal{I}_{\varepsilon}^{\mathrm{in}}(\varphi,z)|}

\displaystyle\textstyle{=\sum_{\ell=1}^{M}\int_{\operatorname{B}(0,\delta(z)/\varepsilon^{1/2})}\left|\vphantom{\varphi(\Phi_{\ell}^{z}(\varepsilon^{1/2}x))\det(\mathrm{d}\Phi_{\ell}^{z}(\varepsilon^{1/2}x))}\varphi(\Phi_{\ell}^{z}(0))\det(\mathrm{d}\Phi_{\ell}^{z}(0))\right.}

(316)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\textstyle{\left.-\varphi(\Phi_{\ell}^{z}(\varepsilon^{1/2}x))\det(\mathrm{d}\Phi_{\ell}^{z}(\varepsilon^{1/2}x))\right|\exp[-\left\|x\right\|^{2}]\mathrm{d}x/C_{1}\;,}

(317)

which concludes the first part of the proof upon combining this result and the
dominated convergence theorem. Next assume that

\varphi\in\mathrm{C}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d},\mathbb{R})
and for any
\ell\in\{1,\dots,M\}
, let

\chi_{\ell}^{z}:\ \operatorname{B}(0,\delta(z))\to\mathbb{R}
given for any

x\in\operatorname{B}(0,\delta(z))
by

\chi_{\ell}^{z}(x)=\varphi(\Phi_{\ell}^{z}(x))\det(\mathrm{d}\Phi_{\ell}^{z}(x))

(318)

We have that for any
\ell\in\{1,\dots,M\}
,

\chi_{\ell}^{z}\in\mathrm{C}^{1}(\operatorname{B}(0,\delta))
. Therefore, we have that for any

\ell\in\{1,\dots,M\}
and
x\in\operatorname{B}(0,\delta(z))
and
h\in\mathbb{R}^{d}

\mathrm{d}\chi_{\ell}(x)(h)=\mathrm{d}\varphi(\Phi_{\ell}^{z}(x))\mathrm{d}\Phi_{\ell}^{z}(x)(h)\det(\mathrm{d}\Phi_{\ell}^{z}(x))+\varphi(\Phi_{\ell}^{z}(x))\operatorname{Tr}(\mathrm{Adj}(\mathrm{d}\Phi_{\ell}^{z}(x))\mathrm{d}^{2}\Phi_{\ell}^{z}(x)(h))\;.

(319)

Therefore, using
Proposition

B.3
we have that there exist

C\geq 0
and
\upbeta>0
such that for any
\ell\in\{1,\dots,M\}
and

x\in\operatorname{B}(0,\delta(z))
,

\|\mathrm{d}\chi_{\ell}^{z}(x)\|\leq C(1+M_{0,\varphi}+M_{1,\varphi})\sigma^{\star}_{\upbeta}(z)
. Using this result in (
316
) we get that

\textstyle{\left|\mathcal{I}_{0,\varepsilon}^{\mathrm{in}}(\varphi,z)-\mathcal{I}_{\varepsilon}^{\mathrm{in}}(\varphi,z)\right|\leq C\sigma^{\star}_{\upbeta}(z)\varepsilon^{1/2}(1+M_{0,\varphi}+M_{1,\varphi})\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left\|x\right\|\exp[-\left\|x\right\|^{2}]\mathrm{d}x/C_{1}\;,}

(320)

which concludes the proof of (
311
) upon combining this
result, (
314
), and the fact that
\exp[-t]\leq 1/t
for any

t>0
.

Next, we show that there exist
c_{1},\upbeta>0
such that

m(z)\geq c_{1}\min(1/\sigma_{\beta}^{\star}(z),c^{\star}(z))
with

c_{1},\upbeta>0
independent from
z
. Since

\lim_{\|x\|\to+\infty}u(x,z)=+\infty
, there exists
\tilde{x}(z)

which minimizes
x\mapsto u(x,z)
on
\mathsf{E}=\mathbb{R}^{d}\backslash\mathsf{V}(z)
. We
distinguish two cases. If
\tilde{x}(z)\in\mathrm{int}(\mathsf{E})
then
\tilde{x}(z)

is a local minimizer of
x\mapsto u(x,z)
. Hence
m(z)\geq c^{\star}(z)
. If

\tilde{x}(z)\in\mathsf{E}\backslash\mathrm{int}(\mathsf{E})
then

\tilde{x}(z)\in\bar{\mathsf{V}}(z)\backslash\mathsf{V}(z)
and we have that

m(z)\geq\delta(z)^{2}
, which concludes the proof.
∎

Finally using
Lemma

B.1
,

Lemma

B.2
and
Proposition

B.4

we establish our main result.

Proposition B.5
.

Assume
5
. Let
\varphi:\ \mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}
be a

M_{1,\varphi}
-Lipschitz function,
M_{1,\varphi},C_{\varphi}\geq 0
and

z\in\mathsf{Z}
. Assume that
\sigma_{1}^{\star}(z)<+\infty
and that for any

x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
,

\left|\varphi(x)\right|\leq C_{\varphi}\exp[C_{\varphi}\left\|x\right\|^{\upalpha}]
. Then,
there exist
B_{2}\in\mathrm{C}(\mathbb{R}_{+},\mathbb{R}_{+})
and
\upbeta>0
such that

\left|\updelta_{z}\mathrm{S}_{\varepsilon}[\varphi]-\updelta_{z}\mathrm{S}_{0}[\varphi]\right|\leq B_{2}(C_{\varphi})(1+M_{0,\varphi}+M_{1,\varphi})(1+\sigma_{\upbeta}^{\star}(z))\{\varepsilon^{1/2}+\varepsilon^{-d/2}\exp[-c^{\star}(z)/\varepsilon]\}\;,

(321)

with

M_{0,\varphi}=\sup\{|\varphi(x)|\,:\;x\in\mathsf{K}\}
,

\mathsf{K}
,
B_{2}
and
\upbeta
independent from
z
and
B_{2}
non-decreasing.

Proof.

In this proof we assume that
\varphi\in\mathrm{C}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d},\mathbb{R})
. The
extension to Lipschitz function is similar to the proof of

Theorem

3
, see
Section

4.1.3
,
i.e.
we use
the smoothing
Lemma

A.6
. Let

\bar{\varepsilon}\in\left(0,\mathtt{m}/(1+C_{\varphi})\right)
. First, let
\mathsf{V}(z)
be given by

Proposition

B.4
and set
\mathsf{K}
such that

\mathsf{K}=\{x_{0}^{k}(z)\,:\;z\in\mathsf{Z},\ k\in\{1,\dots,N\}\}+\bar{\operatorname{B}}(0,1)\;.

(322)

Note that
\mathsf{V}(z)\subset\mathsf{K}
. Applying

Lemma

B.1
there exists
A_{0}\geq 0
such that for
any
\varepsilon\in\left[0,\bar{\varepsilon}\right]
,

\mathcal{J}_{\varepsilon}(z)\geq A_{0}\;.

(323)

In addition, using
Lemma

B.2
we have that there
exist
\beta_{1}>0
and
A_{1}\in\mathrm{C}(\mathbb{R}_{+},\mathbb{R}_{+})
such that for any

\varepsilon\in\left(0,\bar{\varepsilon}\right)
we have

\mathcal{I}_{\varepsilon}^{\mathrm{out}}(\varphi,z)\leq A_{1}(C_{\varphi})\varepsilon^{-d/2}\{\exp[-m(z)/\varepsilon]+\exp[-\beta_{1}/\varepsilon]\}\;.

(324)

Using
Proposition

B.4
there exist
c_{1},\upgamma>0

(independent from
z
) such that

m(z)\geq\min(c_{1}/\sigma_{\upgamma}^{\star}(z),c^{\star}(z))
. Hence, combining this
result, (
324
) and the fact that there exists
c_{3}
such
that for any
t>0
,
\exp[-1/t]\leq c_{3}t^{(d+1)/2}
, we get

\mathcal{I}_{\varepsilon}^{\mathrm{out}}(\varphi,z)\leq A_{1}(C_{\varphi})\varepsilon^{-d/2}\{\exp[-c^{\star}(z)/\varepsilon]+(\sigma_{\upbeta}^{\star}(z))^{(d+1)/2}\varepsilon^{(d+1)/2}/c_{3}+\exp[-\beta_{1}/\varepsilon]\}\;.

(325)

Therefore, there exist
\upbeta^{\prime}>0
and
A_{2}\in\mathrm{C}(\mathbb{R}_{+},\mathbb{R}_{+})

(non-increasing) such that for any
\varepsilon\in\left(0,\bar{\varepsilon}\right)
we have

\mathcal{I}_{\varepsilon}^{\mathrm{out}}(\varphi,z)\leq A_{2}(C_{\varphi})\{(1+\sigma_{\upbeta^{\prime}}^{\star}(z))\varepsilon^{1/2}+\varepsilon^{-d/2}\exp[-c^{\star}(z)/\varepsilon]\}\;.

(326)

In addition, using
Proposition

B.4
, there exist

B_{1}\geq 0
and
\upbeta^{\prime\prime}>0
such that for any
\varepsilon>0
we have

|\mathcal{I}_{\varepsilon}^{\mathrm{in}}(\varphi,z)-\mathcal{I}_{0}(\varphi,z)|\leq B_{1}\sigma_{\upbeta^{\prime\prime}}^{\star}(z)(1+M_{0,\varphi}(z)+M_{1,\varphi}(z))\varepsilon^{1/2}\;,

(327)

Note that
M_{i,\varphi}(z)\leq M_{i,\varphi}
for
i\in\{0,1\}
. Hence,
combining (
326
) and
(
327
), there exist

A_{3}\in\mathrm{C}(\mathbb{R}_{+},\mathbb{R}_{+})
(non-increasing) and
\upbeta_{0}>0
such that for any

\varepsilon\in\left(0,\bar{\varepsilon}\right)
we have

|\mathcal{I}_{\varepsilon}(\varphi,z)-\mathcal{I}_{0}(\varphi,z)|\leq A_{3}(C_{\varphi})(1+M_{0,\varphi}+M_{1,\varphi})\{(1+\sigma_{\upbeta_{0}}^{\star}(z))\varepsilon^{1/2}+\varepsilon^{-d/2}\exp[-c^{\star}(z)/\varepsilon]\}\;,

(328)

Similar results hold if
\varphi
is replaced by
1
and we get that there
exist
A_{4}\geq 0
,
\upbeta_{1}>0
such that for any

\varepsilon\in\left(0,\bar{\varepsilon}\right)
we have

\displaystyle|\mathcal{I}_{\varepsilon}(\varphi,z)-\mathcal{I}_{0}(\varphi,z)|\leq A_{3}(C_{\varphi})(1+M_{0,\varphi}+M_{1,\varphi})\{(1+\sigma_{\upbeta_{1}}^{\star}(z))\varepsilon^{1/2}+\varepsilon^{-d/2}\exp[-c^{\star}(z)/\varepsilon]\}\;,

(329)

\displaystyle|\mathcal{J}_{\varepsilon}(z)-\mathcal{J}_{0}(z)|\leq A_{4}\{(1+\sigma_{\upbeta_{1}}^{\star}(z))\varepsilon^{1/2}+\varepsilon^{-d/2}\exp[-c^{\star}(z)/\varepsilon]\}\;.

(330)

In addition, we have that

\left|\updelta_{z}\mathrm{S}_{\varepsilon}(\varphi)-\updelta_{z}\mathrm{S}_{0}(\varphi)\right|\leq(\mathcal{I}_{\varepsilon}(\varphi,z)-\mathcal{I}_{0}(\varphi,z))/\mathcal{J}_{\varepsilon}(z)+\mathcal{I}_{0}(\varphi,z)(\mathcal{J}_{0}(z)-\mathcal{J}_{\varepsilon}(z))/(\mathcal{J}_{0}(z)\mathcal{J}_{\varepsilon}(z))\;.

(331)

Finally, we have that

\mathcal{I}_{0}(\varphi,z)\leq M_{0,\varphi}\sigma_{1}^{\star}(z)
. Combining this
result (
323
), (
329
) and
(
331
) concludes the proof.

∎

B.3
Control of the moments

In order to derive the uniform stability of the
limiting measure, we first need to control the moments of

\updelta_{z^{1:n}}\mathrm{S}_{\varepsilon}
uniformly in w.r.t
\varepsilon
,
z
and
n
. Let

z^{1:n}\in\mathsf{Z}^{n}
and
\varepsilon>0
. We consider the Langevin diffusion

(\mathbf{X}_{t}^{\varepsilon}(z^{1:n}))_{t\geq 0}
given by the following Stochastic Differential
Equation (SDE):
\mathbf{X}_{0}^{\varepsilon}(z^{1:n})\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
and

\mathrm{d}\mathbf{X}_{t}^{\varepsilon}(z^{1:n})=-\nabla_{x}U_{n}(\mathbf{X}_{t}^{\varepsilon}(z^{1:n}),z^{1:n})\mathrm{d}t+\sqrt{2\varepsilon}\mathrm{d}\mathbf{B}_{t}\;,

(332)

where
(\mathbf{B}_{t})_{t\geq 0}
is a
d
-dimensional Brownian motion with filtration

(\mathcal{F}_{t})_{t\geq 0}
. We recall that for any
n\in\mathbb{N}
,

z^{1:n}\in\mathsf{Z}^{n}
and
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
we have

U_{n}(x,z^{1:n})=(1/n)\sum_{i=1}^{n}u(x,z_{i})
. Therefore, under

3
(
n
) we have that
(\mathbf{X}_{t}^{\varepsilon}(z^{1:n}))_{t\geq 0}
is
well-defined and admits
\updelta_{z^{1:n}}\mathrm{S}_{\varepsilon}
as an invariant
measure, see
[
61
]
for instance.

Lemma B.6
.

Let
n\in\mathbb{N}
and assume
3
(
n
)
and

4
(
n
)
. Let
z^{1:n}\in\mathsf{Z}^{n}
and assume that

\sigma_{1}^{\star}(z^{1:n})<+\infty
. Then there exist
\bar{\varepsilon}>0
such that
for any
k\in\mathbb{N}
there exists
C_{k}\geq 0
such that for any

\varepsilon\in\left[0,\bar{\varepsilon}\right)

\textstyle{\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left\|x\right\|^{2k}\mathrm{S}_{\varepsilon}(z^{1:n},\mathrm{d}x)\leq C_{k}\;,}

(333)

with
C_{k}
and
\bar{\varepsilon}
independent from
n\in\mathbb{N}
and
z^{1:n}\in\mathsf{Z}^{n}
.

Proof.

Let
\varepsilon>0
. First, since
x\mapsto\nabla_{x}U_{n}(x,z^{1:n})
is
Lipschitz continuous we have that
(\mathbf{X}_{t}(z^{1:n})^{\varepsilon})_{t\geq 0}
is
well-defined and is a continuous semi-martingale using
[
45
, Theorem 2.3,
Theorem 2.4 , Chapter 4]
such that for any
t\geq 0

\textstyle{\mathbf{X}_{t}^{\varepsilon}(z^{1:n})=\mathbf{X}_{0}^{\varepsilon}(z^{1:n})-\int_{0}^{t}\nabla_{x}U_{n}(\mathbf{X}_{s}^{\varepsilon}(z^{1:n}),z^{1:n})\mathrm{d}s+\sqrt{2\varepsilon}\mathbf{B}_{t}\;.}

(334)

Let
\mathtt{m}_{0}>0
. Using Itô’s formula, see
[
45
, Theorem
5.1]
we have that for any
t\geq 0
and
k\in\mathbb{N}

with
k\geq 2

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{X}_{t}^{\varepsilon}(z^{1:n})\|^{2k}\exp[\mathtt{m}_{0}t]=\textstyle{\mathbf{X}_{0}^{\varepsilon}(z^{1:n})+2k\int_{0}^{t}\langle\mathbf{X}_{s}^{\varepsilon}(z^{1:n}),\nabla_{x}U_{n}(\mathbf{X}_{s}^{\varepsilon}(z^{1:n}),z^{1:n})\rangle\|\mathbf{X}_{s}^{\varepsilon}(z^{1:n})\|^{2(k-1)}\exp[\mathtt{m}_{0}s]\mathrm{d}s}

(335)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\textstyle{+\mathtt{m}_{0}\int_{0}^{t}\|\mathbf{X}_{s}^{\varepsilon}(z^{1:n})\|^{2k}\exp[\mathtt{m}_{0}s]\mathrm{d}s+4\varepsilon k(2k-1)\int_{0}^{t}\|\mathbf{X}_{s}^{\varepsilon}(z^{1:n})\|^{2(k-1)}\exp[\mathtt{m}_{0}s]\mathrm{d}s+\mathbf{M}_{t}^{\varepsilon}(z^{1:n})\;,}

(336)

with
(\mathbf{M}_{t}^{\varepsilon}(z^{1:n}))_{t\geq 0}
a
\mathcal{F}_{t}
-martingale such
that
\mathbf{M}_{0}^{\varepsilon}(z^{1:n})=0
. First, using Fubini-Tonelli theorem, we have that for any
t\geq 0
and
k\in\mathbb{N}

\displaystyle\textstyle{\mathbb{E}[\|\mathbf{X}_{t}^{\varepsilon}(z^{1:n})\|^{2k}]\exp[\mathtt{m}_{0}t]\leq\mathbf{X}_{0}^{\varepsilon}(z^{1:n})+\left|2k\mathtt{m}-\mathtt{m}_{0}\right|\int_{0}^{t}\mathbb{E}[\|\mathbf{X}_{s}^{\varepsilon}(z^{1:n})\|^{2k}]\exp[\mathtt{m}_{0}s]\mathrm{d}s}

(337)

\displaystyle\qquad\textstyle{+(4\varepsilon k(2k-1)+2k\mathtt{c})(1+\int_{0}^{t}\mathbb{E}[\|\mathbf{X}_{s}^{\varepsilon}(z^{1:n})\|^{2k}]\exp[\mathtt{m}_{0}s]\mathrm{d}s)\;.}

(338)

Using Grönwall’s lemma we have that for any
t\geq 0
and
k\in\mathbb{N}
,

\mathbb{E}[\|\mathbf{X}_{t}^{\varepsilon}(z^{1:n})\|^{2k}]<+\infty
. Hence,
using this result, the Fubini theorem and
3
(
n
), we have
that for any
t\geq 0
and
k\in\mathbb{N}

\displaystyle\textstyle{\mathbb{E}[\|\mathbf{X}_{t}^{\varepsilon}(z^{1:n})\|^{2k}]\exp[\mathtt{m}_{0}t]\leq\mathbf{X}_{0}^{\varepsilon}(z^{1:n})-(2k\mathtt{m}-\mathtt{m}_{0})\int_{0}^{t}\mathbb{E}[\|\mathbf{X}_{s}^{\varepsilon}(z^{1:n})\|^{2k}]\exp[\mathtt{m}_{0}s]\mathrm{d}s}

(339)

\displaystyle\qquad\textstyle{+(4\varepsilon k(2k-1)+2k\mathtt{c})\int_{0}^{t}\mathbb{E}[\|\mathbf{X}_{s}^{\varepsilon}(z^{1:n})\|^{2(k-1)}]\exp[\mathtt{m}_{0}s]\mathrm{d}s\;.}

(340)

Combining this result and the fact that for any
t,a,b>0
and

k\in\mathbb{N}
,
at^{2(k-1)}\leq bt^{2k}+a^{k}/b^{k-1}
, we have for any
t\geq 0
and
k\in\mathbb{N}

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\|\mathbf{X}_{t}^{\varepsilon}(z^{1:n})\|^{2k}]\exp[\mathtt{m}_{0}t]

\displaystyle\textstyle{\leq\mathbf{X}_{0}^{\varepsilon}(z^{1:n})-(k\mathtt{m}-\mathtt{m}_{0})\int_{0}^{t}\mathbb{E}[\|\mathbf{X}_{s}^{\varepsilon}(z^{1:n})\|^{2k}]\exp[\mathtt{m}_{0}s]\mathrm{d}s}

(341)

\displaystyle\quad+(4\varepsilon k(2k-1)+2k\mathtt{c})^{k}/((k\mathtt{m})^{k-1}\mathtt{m}_{0})\exp[\mathtt{m}_{0}t]\;.

(342)

Therefore, for any
k\in\mathbb{N}
, there exists
C_{k}\geq 0
such that for any

t\geq 0
,
\mathbb{E}[\|\mathbf{X}_{t}^{\varepsilon}(z^{1:n})\|^{2k}]\leq C_{k}

upon letting
\mathtt{m}_{0}=k\mathtt{m}
in (
341
) with
C_{k}
independent
from
\varepsilon
and
z^{1:n}
. Therefore, using that the sequence of
distributions associated with
(\mathbf{X}_{t}^{\varepsilon}(z^{1:n}))_{t\geq 0}
weakly
converges towards
\updelta_{z^{1:n}}\mathrm{S}_{\varepsilon}
, see

[
61
]
for instance, and the monotone convergence theorem we get that

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left\|x\right\|^{2k}\mathrm{S}_{\varepsilon}(z^{1:n},\mathrm{d}x)\leq C_{k}
. We
conclude upon using
Proposition

B.5
in the case where

\varepsilon=0
.
∎

Finally, we will make use of the following lemma.

Lemma B.7
.

Let
\mu,\nu\in\mathscr{P}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
such that for any
k\in\mathbb{N}

there exists
C_{k}\geq 0
such that

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\|x\|^{2k}\mathrm{d}\mu(x)+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\|x\|^{2k}\mathrm{d}\nu(x)\leq C_{k}
. Then, for any
k\in\mathbb{N}
we have that

\mathbf{W}_{2}(\mu,\nu)\leq\sqrt{2C_{k}}\mathbf{W}_{1}(\mu,\nu)^{(k-1)/(2k-1)}\;.

(343)

Proof.

Let
k\in\mathbb{N}
and
(X,Y)
be the optimal coupling between
\mu
and
\nu

w.r.t. the Wasserstein distance of order one. Let
Z=X-Y
. Using
Hölder’s inequality we have

\textstyle{\mathbb{E}[\|Z\|^{2}]\leq\mathbb{E}[\|Z\|^{2k}]^{1/(2k-1)}\mathbb{E}[\|Z\|]^{(2k-2)/(2k-1)}\leq\mathbb{E}[\|Z\|^{2k}]^{1/(2k-1)}\mathbf{W}_{1}(\mu,\nu)^{(2k-2)/(2k-1)}\;.}

(344)

We conclude upon combining this result and the fact that

\mathbb{E}[\|Z\|^{2k}]^{1/(2k-1)}\leq 2C_{k}
.
∎

Appendix C
Basics on flows and geometric measure theory

In this section, we recall basic facts from geometric measure theory. We refer to

[
29
,
54
,
1
]
for a
complete exposition of geometric measure theory concepts. We begin with a
proposition establishing the existence of flows. Then, we prove useful facts on
rectifiable sets. Finally, we state area and coarea formulas which are central to
our analysis.

Lemma C.1
.

Let
k\in\mathbb{N}^{\star}
and

X\in\mathrm{C}^{k}(\mathbb{R}^{d},\mathbb{R}^{d})
. Assume that
X
is compactly supported. Then, there
exists a unique mapping
\Phi\in\mathrm{C}^{k+1,k}(\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{d},\mathbb{R}^{d})
such that for
any
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
,
\Phi(0,x)=x
and
\partial_{s}\Phi(s,\mathbb{R}^{d})=X(\Phi(s,\mathbb{R}^{d}))
. In
addition, for any
s\in\mathbb{R}
,
x\mapsto\Phi(s,x)
is a diffeomorphism.

Proof.

The first part of the proposition is an application of
[
49
, Theorem 2.5,
Theorem 2.6]
. The second part is an application of

[
53
, Lemma 2.4]
.
∎

Note that the previous lemma can be extended to smooth manifolds.

Definition C.2
.

[
1
, Definition 2.57]

Let
\mathsf{E}\subset\mathbb{R}^{d}
and
k\in\mathbb{N}
with
k\leq d
.
\mathsf{E}
is
countably
\mathcal{H}^{k}
-rectifiable if there exists
(\psi_{i})_{i\in\mathbb{N}}
such
that for any
i\in\mathbb{N}
,
\psi_{i}:\ \mathbb{R}^{k}\to\mathbb{R}^{d}
is Lipschitz
continuous and

\mathcal{H}^{k}\left(\mathsf{E}\backslash\cup_{i\in\mathbb{N}}\psi_{i}(\mathbb{R}^{k})\right)=0\;.

(345)

In what follows, we provide an easy criterion to verify if a given level set is
a countably
\mathcal{H}^{k}
-rectifiable set. We start by recalling the following
proposition.

Proposition C.3
.

[
50
, Theorem 10.5]

Let
k\in\mathbb{N}
with
k\leq d
and
F:\ \mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}^{k}
be Lipschitz continuous. Then
for
\lambda
-almost every
y\in\mathbb{R}^{k}
,
F^{-1}(\{y\})
is countably

\mathcal{H}^{d-k}
-rectifiable.

The following lemma ensures the stability of countably

\mathcal{H}^{k}
-rectifiable sets through diffeomorphisms.

Lemma C.4
.

Let
k\in\mathbb{N}
with
k\leq d
,
\mathsf{E}\subset\mathbb{R}^{d}
be a compact
countably
\mathcal{H}^{k}
-rectifiable set and
\Phi\in\mathrm{C}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d},\mathbb{R}^{d})
be
a diffeomorphism. Then
\Phi(\mathsf{E})
is countably
\mathcal{H}^{k}
-rectifiable.

Proof.

Since
\mathsf{E}\subset\mathbb{R}^{d}
is countably
\mathcal{H}^{k}
-rectifiable there exists

(\psi_{i})_{i\in\mathbb{N}}
such that for any
i\in\mathbb{N}
,

\psi_{i}:\ \mathbb{R}^{k}\to\mathbb{R}^{d}
is Lipschitz continuous and

\mathcal{H}^{k}\left(\mathsf{E}\backslash\cup_{i\in\mathbb{N}}\psi_{i}(\mathbb{R}^{k})\right)=0\;.

(346)

Let
y\in\Phi(\mathsf{E})\backslash\cup_{i\in\mathbb{N}}(\Phi\circ\psi_{i})(\mathbb{R}^{k})
. Then there exists
x\in\mathsf{E}
such that
y=\Phi(x)
and for
any
i\in\mathbb{N}
and
y_{i}\in\psi_{i}(\mathbb{R}^{k})
,
\Phi(x)\neq\Phi(y_{i})
,
i.e.

x\neq y_{i}
. Therefore

\Phi(\mathsf{E})\backslash\cup_{i\in\mathbb{N}}(\Phi\circ\psi_{i})(\mathbb{R}^{k})\subset\Phi(\mathsf{E}\backslash\cup_{i\in\mathbb{N}}\psi_{i}(\mathbb{R}^{k}))
.
\Phi
is
Lipschitz-continuous on
\mathsf{E}
with constant
\mathtt{L}_{\mathsf{E}}\geq 0
and therefore
using
[
1
, Proposition 2.49 (iv)]
we get

0\leq\mathcal{H}^{k}\left(\Phi(\mathsf{E})\backslash\cup_{i\in\mathbb{N}}(\Phi\circ\psi_{i})(\mathbb{R}^{k}))\right)\leq\mathtt{L}_{\mathsf{E}}^{k}\mathcal{H}^{k}\left(\mathsf{E}\backslash\cup_{i\in\mathbb{N}}\psi_{i}(\mathbb{R}^{k})\right)\leq 0\;,

(347)

which concludes the proof.
∎

Finally, we show that if a function is regular enough then its level-sets are
countably
\mathcal{H}^{k}
-rectifiable.

Lemma C.5
.

Let
F\in\mathrm{C}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d},\mathbb{R}^{p})
with
d\geq p
and

\mathsf{A}\subset\mathbb{R}^{p}
compact such that
F^{-1}(\mathsf{A})
is compact and

0\in\mathrm{int}(\mathsf{A})
. In addition, assume that for any

x\in F^{-1}(\{0\})
,
|\mathrm{J}F(x)|>0
. Then, there exists
\eta>0

such that for any
t\in\bar{\operatorname{B}}_{\infty}(0,\eta)
,
F^{-1}(t)
is countably

\mathcal{H}^{d-p}
-rectifiable and
\mathcal{H}^{d-p}(F^{-1}(t))<+\infty
.

Proof.

In this proof, we show that the level sets
F^{-1}(t)
and
F^{-1}(s)
are
diffeomorphic for
t,s\in\bar{\operatorname{B}}(0,\eta)
. Then, we conclude upon combining

Proposition

C.3
and
Lemma

C.4
. First, note that

F^{-1}(0)
is compact since it is closed in
F^{-1}(\mathsf{A})
which is
compact. There exists
\eta_{0}>0
such that for any

x\in F^{-1}(\bar{\operatorname{B}}_{\infty}(0,\eta_{0}))
,
\left|\mathrm{J}F(x)\right|>0
. Indeed, if this
is not the case then there exists
(x_{k})_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
with

\lim_{k\to+\infty}F(x_{k})=0
and
\left|\mathrm{J}F(x_{k})\right|=0
. Since

0\in\mathrm{int}(\mathsf{A})
there exists
\eta^{\prime}>0
such that

F^{-1}(\bar{\operatorname{B}}(0,\eta^{\prime}))
is compact and therefore there exists
x^{\star}

such that, up to extraction,
\lim_{k\to+\infty}x_{k}=x^{\star}
. Then

F(x^{\star})=0
and
\mathrm{J}F(x^{\star})=0
, which is absurd. We define

\mathsf{K}_{0}=F^{-1}(\bar{\operatorname{B}}_{\infty}(0,\eta_{0}))
and

\mathsf{K}_{1}=F^{-1}(\bar{\operatorname{B}}_{\infty}(0,\eta))
with
\eta=\min(\eta_{0}/2,\eta^{\prime})
. Note that
\mathsf{K}_{1}\subset\mathrm{int}(\mathsf{K}_{0})
.

Let
G(x)=\mathrm{D}F(x)\mathrm{D}F(x)^{\top}
. Note that for any
x\in\mathsf{K}_{0}
,

G(x)
is invertible. We define
\{f_{i}\}_{i=1}^{p}
such that for
any
i\in\{1,\dots,p\}
,
f_{i}:\ \mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}^{d}
with for any

x\in\mathsf{K}_{0}

\textstyle{f_{i}(x)=\sum_{k=1}^{p}h_{i,k}(x)\nabla F_{k}(x)\;,}

(348)

with
\{h_{i,j}(x)\}_{1\leq i,j\leq p}=G^{-1}(x)
. In addition, we
assume that
f_{i}(x)=0
for any
x\notin\mathsf{K}_{1}
. For any
x\in\mathsf{K}_{1}

and
i,j\in\{1,\dots,p\}
we have

\textstyle{\langle f_{i}(x),\nabla F_{j}(x)\rangle=\sum_{k=1}^{p}h_{i,k}(x)\langle\nabla F_{k}(x),\nabla F_{j}(x)\rangle=\updelta_{i}(j)\;.}

(349)

In what follows, we let
\{g_{i}\}_{i=1}^{p}
such that for any

i\in\{1,\dots,p\}
,
g_{i}:\ \mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}^{d}
and

g_{i}\in\mathrm{C}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d},\mathbb{R}^{d})
such that for any
x\in\mathsf{K}_{1}
,

g_{i}(x)=f_{i}(x)
and for any
x\notin\mathrm{int}(\mathsf{K}_{1})
,
g_{i}(x)=0
, such
functions exist using Whitney extension theorem for instance, see

[
69
]
. In what follows, we fix

t=(t_{0},\dots,t_{p})\in\bar{\operatorname{B}}_{\infty}(0,\eta)
. For any

i\in\{1,\dots,p\}
let
\Phi_{i}:\ \mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}^{d}

given by
\Phi_{i}(0,\cdot)=\operatorname{Id}
and for any
s\in\mathbb{R}
and

x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}

\partial_{s}\Phi_{i}(s,x)=-g_{i}(\Phi_{i}(s,x))\;.

(350)

For any
i\in\{1,\dots,p\}
,
\Phi_{i}
is well-defined using

Lemma

C.1
. Therefore, we have for any
i\in\{1,\dots,p\}
and

s\in\mathbb{R}
,
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
such that
\Phi(s,x)\in\mathsf{K}_{1}

\partial_{s}F(\Phi_{i}(s,x))=-(\langle g_{i}(\Phi_{i}(s,x)),\nabla F_{1}(\Phi_{i}(s,x))\rangle,\dots,\langle g_{i}(\Phi_{i}(s,x)),\nabla F_{p}(\Phi_{i}(s,x))\rangle)=-e_{i}\;,

(351)

where we recall that
\{e_{i}\}_{i=1}^{p}
is the canonical basis of

\mathbb{R}^{p}
. We define
\bar{\Phi}_{t}:\ \mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}^{d}
such that for any

x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
,
\bar{\Phi}_{t}(x)=x^{(p)}
with
x^{(0)}=x
and for any

i\in\{0,\dots,p-1\}
,
x^{(i+1)}=\Phi_{i+1}(t_{i+1},x^{(i)})
. Note that

\bar{\Phi}_{t}\in\mathrm{C}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d},\mathbb{R})
is a diffeomorphism using

Lemma

C.1
. Using
Proposition

C.3
, there exists

t_{0}\in\bar{\operatorname{B}}_{\infty}(0,\eta,
) such that
F^{-1}(t_{0})
is countably

\mathcal{H}^{d-p}
rectifiable. Let
s\in\bar{\operatorname{B}}_{\infty}(0,\eta)
. Using

Lemma

C.4
and that
\bar{\Phi}_{-s}\circ\bar{\Phi}_{t_{0}}

is a diffeomorphism between
F^{-1}(t_{0})
and
F^{-1}(s)
we get that

F^{-1}(s)
is countably
\mathcal{H}^{d-p}
rectifiable, which concludes the
first part of the proof.

For the second part of the proof, we let
R\geq 0
such that

F^{-1}(\mathsf{A})\subset\bar{\operatorname{B}}(0,R)
. Since
\bar{\operatorname{B}}(0,R)
is
\mathcal{H}^{d}
-rectifiable
we obtain using the coarea formula
Theorem

C.6

\textstyle{\int_{\bar{\operatorname{B}}(0,R)}|\mathrm{J}F(x)|\mathrm{d}x=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{p}}\mathcal{H}^{d-p}(\bar{\operatorname{B}}(0,R)\cap F^{-1}(t))\mathrm{d}t\geq\int_{\bar{\operatorname{B}}_{\infty}(0,\eta)}\mathcal{H}^{d-p}(F^{-1}(t))\mathrm{d}t\;.}

(352)

Therefore, there exists
t_{0}\in\bar{\operatorname{B}}_{\infty}(0,\eta)
such that

\mathcal{H}^{d-p}(F^{-1}(t_{0}))<+\infty
. Let

\Psi=\bar{\Phi}_{-t}\circ\bar{\Phi}_{t_{0}}
and
\mathtt{L}\geq 0
such that
for any
x\in F^{-1}(t_{0})
we have
\left\|\mathrm{d}\Psi(x)\right\|\leq\mathtt{L}
. Then,
using
[
1
, Proposition 2.49 (iv)]
we have

\mathcal{H}^{d-p}(F^{-1}(t))=\mathcal{H}^{d-p}(\Psi(F^{-1}(t_{0})))\leq\mathtt{L}^{d-p}\mathcal{H}^{d-p}(F^{-1}(t_{0}))<+\infty\;,

(353)

which concludes the proof.
∎

We conclude this section with the area and coarea formulae.

Theorem C.6
.

Let
F:\mathrm{C}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d},\mathbb{R}^{p})
be Lipschitz continuous,
k\in\mathbb{N}
with

k\leq d
and
\mathsf{E}\subset\mathbb{R}^{d}
be a countably
\mathcal{H}^{k}
-rectifiable
set. Let
\varphi:\ \mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}
measurable such that

\textstyle{\int_{\mathsf{E}}\left|\varphi(x)\right|\mathrm{J}F(x)\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{k}(x)<+\infty\;,}

(354)

or assume that
\varphi:\ \mathbb{R}^{d}\to\left[0,+\infty\right)
. Then, the following hold:

•

(Area formula) If
d\leq p
then

\textstyle{\int_{\mathsf{E}}\varphi(x)\mathrm{J}F(x)\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{k}(x)=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{p}}\left(\int_{\mathsf{E}\cap F^{-1}(y)}\varphi(x)\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{0}(x)\right)\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{k}(y)\;.}

(355)

•

(Coarea formula) If
k\geq p
then

\textstyle{\int_{\mathsf{E}}\varphi(x)\mathrm{J}F(x)\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{k}(x)=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{p}}\left(\int_{\mathsf{E}\cap F^{-1}(y)}\varphi(x)\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{k-p}(x)\right)\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{p}(y)\;.}

(356)

Proof.

These results follow from
[
1
, Theorem 2.91, Theorem
2.93]
combined with
[
1
, Exercicse
2.12]
.
∎

In particular, note that if
F\in\mathrm{C}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d},\mathbb{R}^{d})
is a Lipschitz
diffeomorphism we have using
Theorem

C.6
that for any

\varphi:\ \mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}
measurable such that

\textstyle{\int_{\mathsf{E}}\left|\varphi(x)\right|\left|J\Phi(x)\right|\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{k}(x)<+\infty\;,}

(357)

the following change of variable formula with respect to
\mathcal{H}^{k}
holds

\textstyle{\int_{\mathsf{E}}\varphi(x)\mathrm{J}F(x)\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{k}(x)=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left(\int_{\mathsf{E}\cap F^{-1}(y)}\varphi(x)\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{0}(x)\right)\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{k}(y)=\int_{F(\mathsf{E})}\varphi(F^{-1}(y))\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{k}(y)\;.}

(358)

Note that in order for (
358
) to hold,
\mathsf{E}
needs to be
countably
\mathcal{H}^{k}
-rectifiable.
